But in the moment, it feels really true.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been times when you were completely sure about something happening — like being watched or people trying to get you in trouble — even when others told you there's no evidence or reason to think that way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… yes. That happens at work mostly. I’ll be ringing things in and I get really sure that a couple of the other servers are watching what I’m doing, like waiting for me to mess up. And there’s this regular customer too, he’s just always there, and I start feeling like he’s noticing everything.\n\nThen I’ll see little marks on the tickets or something written weird, and it feels like they’re leaving signs. Like they’re proving I did something wrong so they can get me fired.\n\nMy husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, like tickets get messy and people scribble things, and maybe that’s true, but when I’m in it, I don’t feel unsure. I feel like it’s obvious. Like they’re doing it on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe one of these instances where you felt certain someone was trying to get you in trouble — like the marks on the tickets — and tell me whether you think that belief makes sense to someone else, or if it’s something only you would understand?\n\nPatient: Yeah… one time I was closing out a table and the ticket had, like, these little marks near the total, and one of the servers had been standing kind of close to the register. Not doing anything obvious, just… close. And then that regular guy was there too, the one who sits with coffee forever, and I started thinking they were all watching how I rang it in.\n\nIt felt like the marks were there to show I’d done something wrong, like they were collecting proof so management could fire me. And I know if I say that to my husband, he just says, “That’s restaurant stuff, people scribble on tickets,” and he’s probably… I mean, maybe to someone else it sounds like nothing.\n\nBut to me, when I’m there, it connects. The marks, the way people look over, the customer staying so long. It makes sense in my head at the time, even if later I can hear how it probably sounds like only I would understand it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or ideas that you know most people would find very strange or impossible, like something that could never happen in real life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say impossible, like… I’m not saying there’s magic or anything. It’s more like at work, it feels like people are doing things on purpose that everyone else would probably say is just normal.\n\nLike those tickets — little marks, weird notes near the totals — and then the servers standing near the register, and that regular customer just sitting there watching. To me it feels like they’re leaving signs, like a trail, to show I rang something in wrong so they can get me fired.\n\nAnd I know my husband would say, “That’s not a real thing, nobody’s doing that, it’s just restaurant mess-ups,” and maybe most people would think that sounds strange. But when I’m there, in the middle of it, it feels real. Like I’m the only one noticing it.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was happening that you know most people would think is impossible or doesn’t happen in real life?\n\nPatient: I guess… not impossible like flying or something. But, yeah, I keep getting this feeling at work that a couple of the servers and this one regular customer are watching how I ring things in. Like they’re not just looking over by accident.\n\nAnd then if there are little marks or weird notes on the tickets, I start thinking they’re leaving signs, like proof that I did something wrong so they can get me fired. My husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, people mess up tickets all the time, and I know most people would probably say that doesn’t really happen that way.\n\nBut when I’m there, it feels like it’s happening. It feels obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that you yourself would describe as completely outside the realm of normal possibility — something that no one else could ever experience or that goes entirely against how the world works?\n\nPatient: Completely outside… no, I don’t think like, laws of the universe or whatever. I’m not saying something impossible like that. It’s more… at work, I keep feeling like a couple of the servers and that one regular customer are watching me when I ring things in, and then there’ll be these little marks on the tickets, like they’re leaving signs or keeping proof so I get in trouble.\n\nAnd I know my husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, like people scribble on tickets and mistakes happen, and he makes it sound like I’m turning it into something. But when I’m there, it feels connected. Like the ticket, the way someone stands near the register, the customer just sitting with his coffee forever… I mean, he always orders the same thing too, which shouldn’t matter, I know, but it starts to feel like it does.\n\nSo not impossible exactly. Just something most people probably wouldn’t see the way I do.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was happening that you yourself would describe as completely outside the realm of normal possibility — something that no one else could ever experience or that goes entirely against how the world works?\n\nPatient: I mean… completely outside normal possibility, no, not like something nobody could ever experience. It’s not like I think the world stopped working or anything.\n\nIt’s more that at work I get stuck on this thing with the tickets. Like I’ll ring something in, and then there’ll be a little mark near the total, or some note that doesn’t really need to be there, and one of the servers will be hanging around the register. And there’s this regular customer too, he’s there a lot, and I start feeling like they’re watching how I do it, like they’re leaving little signs to prove I messed up so they can get me fired.\n\nMy husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, you know, tickets get messy, people scribble things, mistakes happen. And I can hear that when he says it. But when I’m actually there, it doesn’t feel normal. It feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if any of these beliefs you’ve had—like people leaving signs on tickets or watching you at work—felt like something that could happen in real life, or did they feel more like something unusual or not based on how things normally work?\n\nPatient: It feels like it could happen, I guess. Like… people do watch each other at work, and people do write things on tickets, so it’s not like magic or anything. But then it gets bigger in my head.\n\nLike if one of the servers is near the register, and there’s some weird mark on a ticket, I start thinking, okay, they’re tracking what I’m doing, they’re leaving proof so later they can say I messed up and get me fired. And there’s that regular customer too, just sitting there, and I know customers sit around all the time, but with him it feels like he’s part of it somehow.\n\nMy husband keeps saying it’s just normal restaurant mess-ups, and maybe it is, but when I’m in the middle of it, it doesn’t feel normal. It feels really pointed at me.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been times when you were absolutely certain about something happening—like people trying to get you in trouble—even though there was no real evidence, and it turned out not to be true at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, there have been times I felt really sure. Like at work, with the servers and the tickets. I’ll see a mark near the total or something written in a way that seems unnecessary, and if one of them is nearby, it feels like they’re keeping track of me, like they’re making a little record so later they can say I did something wrong and get me fired.\n\nAnd there’s this regular customer who’s there a lot, and I know that sounds kind of dumb because people are allowed to sit and drink coffee, obviously, but he just… he watches, or it feels like he watches. It reminds me of when I was in school and people would pass notes, and you knew it was about you even if nobody said your name—sorry, that’s not really the same thing.\n\nMy husband says there’s no real evidence, that tickets get marked up all the time and restaurants are messy and fast. And later, sometimes, I can see that maybe nothing actually happened. But in the moment, I’m pretty convinced it’s all connected and meant to get me in trouble.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you think your concerns about people watching you or leaving signs on tickets are actually happening in real life, or if they’re more about how you feel in the moment, even if they might not make sense to someone else?\n\nPatient: I think… when I’m calm, like at home talking to my husband, I can say maybe it’s just how it feels in the moment. Like restaurants are chaotic, tickets get marked up, people stand around the register because that’s where things happen.\n\nBut when I’m actually there, it feels real. Like a couple of the servers are watching how I ring things in, and then there’ll be a little mark on the ticket or some note, and I’ll think, see, that’s not random. That’s them leaving signs, like proof, so later they can say I did something wrong and get me fired.\n\nAnd there’s this regular customer who’s always there, or it feels like he’s always there, and I know regulars exist—my mom used to go to the same diner every Thursday with her friend, so I know people have routines—but with him it feels like he’s part of it too. My husband says it’s just normal mess-ups and I’m making connections that aren’t there, and maybe someone else would see it that way. But in the moment, it really feels like it’s happening.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah… I don’t know if it’s “very unusual” like, not real-life unusual, because it feels like it could be real. But I guess other people would probably think it sounds… hard to believe.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22.0, F23.8, F25.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. I’m a waitress, and during service I’ve been sort of freezing up, like just stopping in the middle of everything.\n\nThere was this one time I was by the soda station holding the cash drawer key, and I was supposed to be closing checks, but I just kept repeating table numbers. It’s embarrassing because I know how to do my job—I’ve done it a long time—and then suddenly it’s like the steps get mixed up.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're seeing or hearing things that others don't, or had thoughts that others might find unusual or not based in reality?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, I mean, I don’t love saying it like that, because it sounds worse when you say it out loud. It’s happened at home too, in my kitchen, which is weird because my kitchen has that old refrigerator noise, and I used to think maybe it was that, or the pipes. Not big signs, just… enough that I notice. And I’ll think, okay, they’re trying to build a case to get me fired.\n\nMy husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, like tickets getting messy or people being careless, and restaurants are always chaos anyway. Which is true, I guess, like the printer jams and people spill syrup in places you don’t even understand. But still, when I see it, it feels like it’s meant for me. Like they’re letting me know they know.\n\nDoctor: Are there times when you feel certain about something happening — like being watched or that people are trying to get you in trouble — even when others tell you there's no evidence or reason to think that way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s pretty much it. I’ll be at the register, and I get this feeling that a couple of the servers are watching exactly how I ring things in. Like proof, sort of, that they’re trying to show I messed up so they can get me fired.\n\nMy husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, tickets get messy, people make mistakes, and I know restaurants are chaotic. But when I’m there, it feels very directed at me. Like I’m certain for a while, even if later I can kind of hear how it sounds.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that you know are strange or not based in reality, even if they feel true to you at the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I know it sounds strange when I’m saying it here, like out loud in an office. But at work it doesn’t feel strange. It feels like, no, I’m seeing what’s happening.\n\nLike with the register stuff — I keep feeling like a couple of the servers are watching how I ring things in, and then there’s this one regular who just sits there forever, and I start thinking he’s part of it too. Then I’ll see little marks on the tickets or something written in a weird way, and it feels like they’re leaving signs, like they’re trying to prove I messed up so they can get me fired.\n\nMy husband keeps saying it’s just restaurant mess, people scribble things, tickets get messed up, nobody’s doing secret messages… and I can kind of understand that later. But in the moment, it feels really true.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been times when you were completely sure about something happening — like being watched or people trying to get you in trouble — even when others told you there's no evidence or reason to think that way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… yes. That happens at work mostly. I’ll be ringing things in and I get really sure that a couple of the other servers are watching what I’m doing, like waiting for me to mess up. And there’s this regular customer too, he’s just always there, and I start feeling like he’s noticing everything.\n\nThen I’ll see little marks on the tickets or something written weird, and it feels like they’re leaving signs. Like they’re proving I did something wrong so they can get me fired.\n\nMy husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, like tickets get messy and people scribble things, and maybe that’s true, but when I’m in it, I don’t feel unsure. I feel like it’s obvious. Like they’re doing it on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe one of these instances where you felt certain someone was trying to get you in trouble — like the marks on the tickets — and tell me whether you think that belief makes sense to someone else, or if it’s something only you would understand?\n\nPatient: Yeah… one time I was closing out a table and the ticket had, like, these little marks near the total, and one of the servers had been standing kind of close to the register. Not doing anything obvious, just… close. And then that regular guy was there too, the one who sits with coffee forever, and I started thinking they were all watching how I rang it in.\n\nIt felt like the marks were there to show I’d done something wrong, like they were collecting proof so management could fire me. And I know if I say that to my husband, he just says, “That’s restaurant stuff, people scribble on tickets,” and he’s probably… I mean, maybe to someone else it sounds like nothing.\n\nBut to me, when I’m there, it connects. The marks, the way people look over, the customer staying so long. It makes sense in my head at the time, even if later I can hear how it probably sounds like only I would understand it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or ideas that you know most people would find very strange or impossible, like something that could never happen in real life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say impossible, like… I’m not saying there’s magic or anything. It’s more like at work, it feels like people are doing things on purpose that everyone else would probably say is just normal.\n\nLike those tickets — little marks, weird notes near the totals — and then the servers standing near the register, and that regular customer just sitting there watching. To me it feels like they’re leaving signs, like a trail, to show I rang something in wrong so they can get me fired.\n\nAnd I know my husband would say, “That’s not a real thing, nobody’s doing that, it’s just restaurant mess-ups,” and maybe most people would think that sounds strange. But when I’m there, in the middle of it, it feels real. Like I’m the only one noticing it.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was happening that you know most people would think is impossible or doesn’t happen in real life?\n\nPatient: I guess… not impossible like flying or something. But, yeah, I keep getting this feeling at work that a couple of the servers and this one regular customer are watching how I ring things in. Like they’re not just looking over by accident.\n\nAnd then if there are little marks or weird notes on the tickets, I start thinking they’re leaving signs, like proof that I did something wrong so they can get me fired. My husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, people mess up tickets all the time, and I know most people would probably say that doesn’t really happen that way.\n\nBut when I’m there, it feels like it’s happening. It feels obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that you yourself would describe as completely outside the realm of normal possibility — something that no one else could ever experience or that goes entirely against how the world works?\n\nPatient: Completely outside… no, I don’t think like, laws of the universe or whatever. I’m not saying something impossible like that. It’s more… at work, I keep feeling like a couple of the servers and that one regular customer are watching me when I ring things in, and then there’ll be these little marks on the tickets, like they’re leaving signs or keeping proof so I get in trouble.\n\nAnd I know my husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, like people scribble on tickets and mistakes happen, and he makes it sound like I’m turning it into something. But when I’m there, it feels connected. Like the ticket, the way someone stands near the register, the customer just sitting with his coffee forever… I mean, he always orders the same thing too, which shouldn’t matter, I know, but it starts to feel like it does.\n\nSo not impossible exactly. Just something most people probably wouldn’t see the way I do.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was happening that you yourself would describe as completely outside the realm of normal possibility — something that no one else could ever experience or that goes entirely against how the world works?\n\nPatient: I mean… completely outside normal possibility, no, not like something nobody could ever experience. It’s not like I think the world stopped working or anything.\n\nIt’s more that at work I get stuck on this thing with the tickets. Like I’ll ring something in, and then there’ll be a little mark near the total, or some note that doesn’t really need to be there, and one of the servers will be hanging around the register. And there’s this regular customer too, he’s there a lot, and I start feeling like they’re watching how I do it, like they’re leaving little signs to prove I messed up so they can get me fired.\n\nMy husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, you know, tickets get messy, people scribble things, mistakes happen. And I can hear that when he says it. But when I’m actually there, it doesn’t feel normal. It feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if any of these beliefs you’ve had—like people leaving signs on tickets or watching you at work—felt like something that could happen in real life, or did they feel more like something unusual or not based on how things normally work?\n\nPatient: It feels like it could happen, I guess. Like… people do watch each other at work, and people do write things on tickets, so it’s not like magic or anything. But then it gets bigger in my head.\n\nLike if one of the servers is near the register, and there’s some weird mark on a ticket, I start thinking, okay, they’re tracking what I’m doing, they’re leaving proof so later they can say I messed up and get me fired. And there’s that regular customer too, just sitting there, and I know customers sit around all the time, but with him it feels like he’s part of it somehow.\n\nMy husband keeps saying it’s just normal restaurant mess-ups, and maybe it is, but when I’m in the middle of it, it doesn’t feel normal. It feels really pointed at me.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been times when you were absolutely certain about something happening—like people trying to get you in trouble—even though there was no real evidence, and it turned out not to be true at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, there have been times I felt really sure. Like at work, with the servers and the tickets. I’ll see a mark near the total or something written in a way that seems unnecessary, and if one of them is nearby, it feels like they’re keeping track of me, like they’re making a little record so later they can say I did something wrong and get me fired.\n\nAnd there’s this regular customer who’s there a lot, and I know that sounds kind of dumb because people are allowed to sit and drink coffee, obviously, but he just… he watches, or it feels like he watches. It reminds me of when I was in school and people would pass notes, and you knew it was about you even if nobody said your name—sorry, that’s not really the same thing.\n\nMy husband says there’s no real evidence, that tickets get marked up all the time and restaurants are messy and fast. And later, sometimes, I can see that maybe nothing actually happened. But in the moment, I’m pretty convinced it’s all connected and meant to get me in trouble.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you think your concerns about people watching you or leaving signs on tickets are actually happening in real life, or if they’re more about how you feel in the moment, even if they might not make sense to someone else?\n\nPatient: I think… when I’m calm, like at home talking to my husband, I can say maybe it’s just how it feels in the moment. Like restaurants are chaotic, tickets get marked up, people stand around the register because that’s where things happen.\n\nBut when I’m actually there, it feels real. Like a couple of the servers are watching how I ring things in, and then there’ll be a little mark on the ticket or some note, and I’ll think, see, that’s not random. That’s them leaving signs, like proof, so later they can say I did something wrong and get me fired.\n\nAnd there’s this regular customer who’s always there, or it feels like he’s always there, and I know regulars exist—my mom used to go to the same diner every Thursday with her friend, so I know people have routines—but with him it feels like he’s part of it too. My husband says it’s just normal mess-ups and I’m making connections that aren’t there, and maybe someone else would see it that way. But in the moment, it really feels like it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that something very unusual was happening—something that you know most people would find hard to believe or that doesn't happen in real life, even if it felt completely true to you at the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I don’t know if it’s “very unusual” like, not real-life unusual, because it feels like it could be real. But I guess other people would probably think it sounds… hard to believe.\n\nAt work I keep thinking a couple of the servers are watching how I ring things in, and then there’ll be these little marks or signs on the tickets, like they’re leaving proof. Like they’re building a case or something so they can say I messed up and get me fired. And there’s this regular customer too, and he’ll be sitting there, and I start feeling like he’s part of it.\n\nMy husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, like tickets get messy and people stand by the register. And I know restaurants are like that—I mean, half the time the printer jams or somebody writes something weird because they’re in a rush—but when I’m there, it feels completely true. Like it’s aimed at me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Yeah… I mean, I guess that’s the part my husband gets frustrated with, because he’ll show me that it’s just a regular ticket mark or that a server writes on everybody’s tickets, not just mine. Later maybe I can calm down and think maybe I overread it, but in the moment, yes, I stay really certain.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave there been times when you believed something was happening that you yourself recognized as not making sense or being something no one else would understand as realistic?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / waiter_or_waitress / some_college\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I keep feeling like a couple of the servers and one of the regular customers are watching how I ring things in and leaving little signs on the tickets to prove they're trying to get me fired, even when my husband says it’s just normal restaurant mess-ups.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I mean, sometimes I do know it sounds like it doesn’t make sense when I say it out loud. Like the thing with the tickets at work—me thinking a couple of the servers, and that one regular customer, are watching how I ring things in and leaving little signs on the tickets so they can prove something and get me fired.

When I’m saying it here, I can hear how someone would be like, “Why would they do all that?” My husband says it’s just normal restaurant mess-ups, like people scribbling on tickets or standing near the register because it’s busy. And he’ll say I’m making it into something bigger.

But when I’m there, it doesn’t feel unrealistic. It feels like… obvious, almost. Like the marks mean something. Sorry, I know I’m kind of going in circles—restaurants are just so hectic, and people do weird little things all shift long—but yeah, there are times I recognize other people wouldn’t understand it as realistic, even if part of me still feels like it’s happening.

Like, out loud. And people were asking me what I was doing, but I couldn’t get it back in the right order. It’s embarrassing because I know how to do my job—I’ve done it a long time—and then suddenly it’s like the steps get mixed up.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're seeing or hearing things that others don't, or had thoughts that others might find unusual or not based in reality?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, I mean, I don’t love saying it like that, because it sounds worse when you say it out loud. But sometimes, especially at closing, when I’m doing the money count, I hear this woman’s voice. Like she’ll say my name, or say, “You’re doing the count wrong,” really clearly.\n\nAnd I’ll answer her sometimes before I catch myself, which is… embarrassing. It’s happened at home too, in my kitchen, which is weird because my kitchen has that old refrigerator noise, and I used to think maybe it was that, or the pipes. Which is true, I guess, like the printer jams and people spill syrup in places you don’t even understand. But still, when I see it, it feels like it’s meant for me. Like they’re letting me know they know.\n\nDoctor: Are there times when you feel certain about something happening — like being watched or that people are trying to get you in trouble — even when others tell you there's no evidence or reason to think that way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s pretty much it. I’ll be at the register, and I get this feeling that a couple of the servers are watching exactly how I ring things in. And there’s this one regular customer too, like he notices too much, even though he’s just sitting there with coffee forever—he always gets coffee, and he leaves the sugar packets in these little stacks, which probably doesn’t matter, but I notice things like that.\n\nThen later I’ll see marks on the tickets, or something written weird, and it feels like they’re leaving little signs. Like proof, sort of, that they’re trying to show I messed up so they can get me fired.\n\nMy husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, tickets get messy, people make mistakes, and I know restaurants are chaotic. But when I’m there, it feels very directed at me. Like I’m certain for a while, even if later I can kind of hear how it sounds.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that you know are strange or not based in reality, even if they feel true to you at the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I know it sounds strange when I’m saying it here, like out loud in an office. Then I’ll see little marks on the tickets or something written in a weird way, and it feels like they’re leaving signs, like they’re trying to prove I messed up so they can get me fired.\n\nMy husband keeps saying it’s just restaurant mess, people scribble things, tickets get messed up, nobody’s doing secret messages… and I can kind of understand that later. But in the moment, it feels really true.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been times when you were completely sure about something happening — like being watched or people trying to get you in trouble — even when others told you there's no evidence or reason to think that way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… yes. That happens at work mostly. I’ll be ringing things in and I get really sure that a couple of the other servers are watching what I’m doing, like waiting for me to mess up. And there’s this regular customer too, he’s just always there, and I start feeling like he’s noticing everything.\n\nThen I’ll see little marks on the tickets or something written weird, and it feels like they’re leaving signs. Like they’re proving I did something wrong so they can get me fired.\n\nMy husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, like tickets get messy and people scribble things, and maybe that’s true, but when I’m in it, I don’t feel unsure. I feel like it’s obvious. Like they’re doing it on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe one of these instances where you felt certain someone was trying to get you in trouble — like the marks on the tickets — and tell me whether you think that belief makes sense to someone else, or if it’s something only you would understand?\n\nPatient: Yeah… one time I was closing out a table and the ticket had, like, these little marks near the total, and one of the servers had been standing kind of close to the register. Not doing anything obvious, just… close. And then that regular guy was there too, the one who sits with coffee forever, and I started thinking they were all watching how I rang it in.\n\nIt felt like the marks were there to show I’d done something wrong, like they were collecting proof so management could fire me. And I know if I say that to my husband, he just says, “That’s restaurant stuff, people scribble on tickets,” and he’s probably… I mean, maybe to someone else it sounds like nothing.\n\nBut to me, when I’m there, it connects. The marks, the way people look over, the customer staying so long. It makes sense in my head at the time, even if later I can hear how it probably sounds like only I would understand it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or ideas that you know most people would find very strange or impossible, like something that could never happen in real life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say impossible, like… I’m not saying there’s magic or anything. It’s more like at work, it feels like people are doing things on purpose that everyone else would probably say is just normal.\n\nLike those tickets — little marks, weird notes near the totals — and then the servers standing near the register, and that regular customer just sitting there watching. To me it feels like they’re leaving signs, like a trail, to show I rang something in wrong so they can get me fired.\n\nAnd I know my husband would say, “That’s not a real thing, nobody’s doing that, it’s just restaurant mess-ups,” and maybe most people would think that sounds strange. But when I’m there, in the middle of it, it feels real. Like I’m the only one noticing it.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was happening that you know most people would think is impossible or doesn’t happen in real life?\n\nPatient: I guess… not impossible like flying or something. But, yeah, I keep getting this feeling at work that a couple of the servers and this one regular customer are watching how I ring things in. Like they’re not just looking over by accident.\n\nAnd then if there are little marks or weird notes on the tickets, I start thinking they’re leaving signs, like proof that I did something wrong so they can get me fired. My husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, people mess up tickets all the time, and I know most people would probably say that doesn’t really happen that way.\n\nBut when I’m there, it feels like it’s happening. It feels obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that you yourself would describe as completely outside the realm of normal possibility — something that no one else could ever experience or that goes entirely against how the world works?\n\nPatient: Completely outside… no, I don’t think like, laws of the universe or whatever. I’m not saying something impossible like that. It’s more… at work, I keep feeling like a couple of the servers and that one regular customer are watching me when I ring things in, and then there’ll be these little marks on the tickets, like they’re leaving signs or keeping proof so I get in trouble.\n\nAnd I know my husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, like people scribble on tickets and mistakes happen, and he makes it sound like I’m turning it into something. But when I’m there, it feels connected. Like the ticket, the way someone stands near the register, the customer just sitting with his coffee forever… I mean, he always orders the same thing too, which shouldn’t matter, I know, but it starts to feel like it does.\n\nSo not impossible exactly. Just something most people probably wouldn’t see the way I do.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was happening that you yourself would describe as completely outside the realm of normal possibility — something that no one else could ever experience or that goes entirely against how the world works?\n\nPatient: I mean… completely outside normal possibility, no, not like something nobody could ever experience. It’s not like I think the world stopped working or anything.\n\nIt’s more that at work I get stuck on this thing with the tickets. Like I’ll ring something in, and then there’ll be a little mark near the total, or some note that doesn’t really need to be there, and one of the servers will be hanging around the register. And there’s this regular customer too, he’s there a lot, and I start feeling like they’re watching how I do it, like they’re leaving little signs to prove I messed up so they can get me fired.\n\nMy husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, you know, tickets get messy, people scribble things, mistakes happen. And I can hear that when he says it. But when I’m actually there, it doesn’t feel normal. Like… people do watch each other at work, and people do write things on tickets, so it’s not like magic or anything. And there’s that regular customer too, just sitting there, and I know customers sit around all the time, but with him it feels like he’s part of it somehow.\n\nMy husband keeps saying it’s just normal restaurant mess-ups, and maybe it is, but when I’m in the middle of it, it doesn’t feel normal. It feels really pointed at me.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been times when you were absolutely certain about something happening—like people trying to get you in trouble—even though there was no real evidence, and it turned out not to be true at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, there have been times I felt really sure. Like at work, with the servers and the tickets. I’ll see a mark near the total or something written in a way that seems unnecessary, and if one of them is nearby, it feels like they’re keeping track of me, like they’re making a little record so later they can say I did something wrong and get me fired.\n\nAnd there’s this regular customer who’s there a lot, and I know that sounds kind of dumb because people are allowed to sit and drink coffee, obviously, but he just… he watches, or it feels like he watches. It reminds me of when I was in school and people would pass notes, and you knew it was about you even if nobody said your name—sorry, that’s not really the same thing.\n\nMy husband says there’s no real evidence, that tickets get marked up all the time and restaurants are messy and fast. And later, sometimes, I can see that maybe nothing actually happened. But in the moment, I’m pretty convinced it’s all connected and meant to get me in trouble.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you think your concerns about people watching you or leaving signs on tickets are actually happening in real life, or if they’re more about how you feel in the moment, even if they might not make sense to someone else?\n\nPatient: I think… when I’m calm, like at home talking to my husband, I can say maybe it’s just how it feels in the moment. Like restaurants are chaotic, tickets get marked up, people stand around the register because that’s where things happen.\n\nBut when I’m actually there, it feels real. Like a couple of the servers are watching how I ring things in, and then there’ll be a little mark on the ticket or some note, and I’ll think, see, that’s not random. That’s them leaving signs, like proof, so later they can say I did something wrong and get me fired.\n\nAnd there’s this regular customer who’s always there, or it feels like he’s always there, and I know regulars exist—my mom used to go to the same diner every Thursday with her friend, so I know people have routines—but with him it feels like he’s part of it too. My husband says it’s just normal mess-ups and I’m making connections that aren’t there, and maybe someone else would see it that way. But in the moment, it really feels like it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that something very unusual was happening—something that you know most people would find hard to believe or that doesn't happen in real life, even if it felt completely true to you at the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I don’t know if it’s “very unusual” like, not real-life unusual, because it feels like it could be real. But I guess other people would probably think it sounds… hard to believe.\n\nAt work I keep thinking a couple of the servers are watching how I ring things in, and then there’ll be these little marks or signs on the tickets, like they’re leaving proof. Like they’re building a case or something so they can say I messed up and get me fired. And there’s this regular customer too, and he’ll be sitting there, and I start feeling like he’s part of it.\n\nMy husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, like tickets get messy and people stand by the register. And I know restaurants are like that—I mean, half the time the printer jams or somebody writes something weird because they’re in a rush—but when I’m there, it feels completely true. Like it’s aimed at me.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been times when you remained completely certain about something happening—like people trying to get you in trouble—even when there was clear evidence it wasn’t true at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess that’s the part my husband gets frustrated with, because he’ll show me that it’s just a regular ticket mark or that a server writes on everybody’s tickets, not just mine. The customer sits there, and then the servers are near the register, and then there’s some little sign or mark on the ticket, and my brain goes, that’s it, they’re making proof so they can get me fired.\n\nEven if my husband says it’s normal restaurant mess-ups—like, he used to work in a pizza place for one summer and he always brings that up, which isn’t the same but okay—when I’m in it, I don’t really believe him. But when I’m there, in the middle of it, it feels real. Like I’m the only one noticing it.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was happening that you know most people would think is impossible or doesn’t happen in real life?\n\nPatient: I guess… not impossible like flying or something. But, yeah, I keep getting this feeling at work that a couple of the servers and this one regular customer are watching how I ring things in. Like they’re not just looking over by accident.\n\nAnd then if there are little marks or weird notes on the tickets, I start thinking they’re leaving signs, like proof that I did something wrong so they can get me fired. My husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, people mess up tickets all the time, and I know most people would probably say that doesn’t really happen that way.\n\nBut when I’m there, it feels like it’s happening. It feels obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that you yourself would describe as completely outside the realm of normal possibility — something that no one else could ever experience or that goes entirely against how the world works?\n\nPatient: Completely outside… no, I don’t think like, laws of the universe or whatever. I’m not saying something impossible like that. It’s more… at work, I keep feeling like a couple of the servers and that one regular customer are watching me when I ring things in, and then there’ll be these little marks on the tickets, like they’re leaving signs or keeping proof so I get in trouble.\n\nAnd I know my husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, like people scribble on tickets and mistakes happen, and he makes it sound like I’m turning it into something. But when I’m there, it feels connected. Like the ticket, the way someone stands near the register, the customer just sitting with his coffee forever… I mean, he always orders the same thing too, which shouldn’t matter, I know, but it starts to feel like it does.\n\nSo not impossible exactly. Just something most people probably wouldn’t see the way I do.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was happening that you yourself would describe as completely outside the realm of normal possibility — something that no one else could ever experience or that goes entirely against how the world works?\n\nPatient: I mean… completely outside normal possibility, no, not like something nobody could ever experience. It’s not like I think the world stopped working or anything.\n\nIt’s more that at work I get stuck on this thing with the tickets. Like I’ll ring something in, and then there’ll be a little mark near the total, or some note that doesn’t really need to be there, and one of the servers will be hanging around the register. And there’s this regular customer too, he’s there a lot, and I start feeling like they’re watching how I do it, like they’re leaving little signs to prove I messed up so they can get me fired.\n\nMy husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, you know, tickets get messy, people scribble things, mistakes happen. And I can hear that when he says it. But when I’m actually there, it doesn’t feel normal. It feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if any of these beliefs you’ve had—like people leaving signs on tickets or watching you at work—felt like something that could happen in real life, or did they feel more like something unusual or not based on how things normally work?\n\nPatient: It feels like it could happen, I guess. Like… people do watch each other at work, and people do write things on tickets, so it’s not like magic or anything. But then it gets bigger in my head.\n\nLike if one of the servers is near the register, and there’s some weird mark on a ticket, I start thinking, okay, they’re tracking what I’m doing, they’re leaving proof so later they can say I messed up and get me fired. And there’s that regular customer too, just sitting there, and I know customers sit around all the time, but with him it feels like he’s part of it somehow.\n\nMy husband keeps saying it’s just normal restaurant mess-ups, and maybe it is, but when I’m in the middle of it, it doesn’t feel normal. It feels really pointed at me.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been times when you were absolutely certain about something happening—like people trying to get you in trouble—even though there was no real evidence, and it turned out not to be true at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, there have been times I felt really sure. Like at work, with the servers and the tickets. I’ll see a mark near the total or something written in a way that seems unnecessary, and if one of them is nearby, it feels like they’re keeping track of me, like they’re making a little record so later they can say I did something wrong and get me fired.\n\nAnd there’s this regular customer who’s there a lot, and I know that sounds kind of dumb because people are allowed to sit and drink coffee, obviously, but he just… he watches, or it feels like he watches. It reminds me of when I was in school and people would pass notes, and you knew it was about you even if nobody said your name—sorry, that’s not really the same thing.\n\nMy husband says there’s no real evidence, that tickets get marked up all the time and restaurants are messy and fast. And later, sometimes, I can see that maybe nothing actually happened. But in the moment, I’m pretty convinced it’s all connected and meant to get me in trouble.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you think your concerns about people watching you or leaving signs on tickets are actually happening in real life, or if they’re more about how you feel in the moment, even if they might not make sense to someone else?\n\nPatient: I think… when I’m calm, like at home talking to my husband, I can say maybe it’s just how it feels in the moment. Like restaurants are chaotic, tickets get marked up, people stand around the register because that’s where things happen.\n\nBut when I’m actually there, it feels real. Like a couple of the servers are watching how I ring things in, and then there’ll be a little mark on the ticket or some note, and I’ll think, see, that’s not random. That’s them leaving signs, like proof, so later they can say I did something wrong and get me fired.\n\nAnd there’s this regular customer who’s always there, or it feels like he’s always there, and I know regulars exist—my mom used to go to the same diner every Thursday with her friend, so I know people have routines—but with him it feels like he’s part of it too. My husband says it’s just normal mess-ups and I’m making connections that aren’t there, and maybe someone else would see it that way. But in the moment, it really feels like it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that something very unusual was happening—something that you know most people would find hard to believe or that doesn't happen in real life, even if it felt completely true to you at the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I don’t know if it’s “very unusual” like, not real-life unusual, because it feels like it could be real. But I guess other people would probably think it sounds… hard to believe.\n\nAt work I keep thinking a couple of the servers are watching how I ring things in, and then there’ll be these little marks or signs on the tickets, like they’re leaving proof. Like they’re building a case or something so they can say I messed up and get me fired. And there’s this regular customer too, and he’ll be sitting there, and I start feeling like he’s part of it.\n\nMy husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, like tickets get messy and people stand by the register. And I know restaurants are like that—I mean, half the time the printer jams or somebody writes something weird because they’re in a rush—but when I’m there, it feels completely true. Like it’s aimed at me.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been times when you remained completely certain about something happening—like people trying to get you in trouble—even when there was clear evidence it wasn’t true at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess that’s the part my husband gets frustrated with, because he’ll show me that it’s just a regular ticket mark or that a server writes on everybody’s tickets, not just mine. The customer sits there, and then the servers are near the register, and then there’s some little sign or mark on the ticket, and my brain goes, that’s it, they’re making proof so they can get me fired.\n\nEven if my husband says it’s normal restaurant mess-ups—like, he used to work in a pizza place for one summer and he always brings that up, which isn’t the same but okay—when I’m in it, I don’t really believe him. Later maybe I can calm down and think maybe I overread it, but in the moment, yes, I stay really certain.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was happening that you yourself recognized as not making sense or being something no one else would understand as realistic?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, sometimes I do know it sounds like it doesn’t make sense when I say it out loud. Completely impossible… I don’t know if I’d call it impossible, because that makes it sound like I know it can’t happen. It’s more like, when I’m at work, it feels completely true, but then later if I explain it, I can hear how it sounds kind of… illogical.

Like proof, sort of, that they’re trying to show I messed up so they can get me fired.\n\nMy husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, tickets get messy, people make mistakes, and I know restaurants are chaotic. But when I’m there, it feels very directed at me. Like I’m certain for a while, even if later I can kind of hear how it sounds.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that you know are strange or not based in reality, even if they feel true to you at the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I know it sounds strange when I’m saying it here, like out loud in an office. But at work it doesn’t feel strange. It feels like, no, I’m seeing what’s happening.\n\nLike with the register stuff — I keep feeling like a couple of the servers are watching how I ring things in, and then there’s this one regular who just sits there forever, and I start thinking he’s part of it too. Then I’ll see little marks on the tickets or something written in a weird way, and it feels like they’re leaving signs, like they’re trying to prove I messed up so they can get me fired.\n\nMy husband keeps saying it’s just restaurant mess, people scribble things, tickets get messed up, nobody’s doing secret messages… and I can kind of understand that later. But in the moment, it feels really true.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been times when you were completely sure about something happening — like being watched or people trying to get you in trouble — even when others told you there's no evidence or reason to think that way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… yes. That happens at work mostly. I’ll be ringing things in and I get really sure that a couple of the other servers are watching what I’m doing, like waiting for me to mess up. And there’s this regular customer too, he’s just always there, and I start feeling like he’s noticing everything.\n\nThen I’ll see little marks on the tickets or something written weird, and it feels like they’re leaving signs. Like they’re proving I did something wrong so they can get me fired.\n\nMy husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, like tickets get messy and people scribble things, and maybe that’s true, but when I’m in it, I don’t feel unsure. I feel like it’s obvious. Like they’re doing it on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe one of these instances where you felt certain someone was trying to get you in trouble — like the marks on the tickets — and tell me whether you think that belief makes sense to someone else, or if it’s something only you would understand?\n\nPatient: Yeah… one time I was closing out a table and the ticket had, like, these little marks near the total, and one of the servers had been standing kind of close to the register. Not doing anything obvious, just… close. And then that regular guy was there too, the one who sits with coffee forever, and I started thinking they were all watching how I rang it in.\n\nIt felt like the marks were there to show I’d done something wrong, like they were collecting proof so management could fire me. And I know if I say that to my husband, he just says, “That’s restaurant stuff, people scribble on tickets,” and he’s probably… I mean, maybe to someone else it sounds like nothing.\n\nBut to me, when I’m there, it connects. The marks, the way people look over, the customer staying so long. It makes sense in my head at the time, even if later I can hear how it probably sounds like only I would understand it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or ideas that you know most people would find very strange or impossible, like something that could never happen in real life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say impossible, like… I’m not saying there’s magic or anything. It’s more like at work, it feels like people are doing things on purpose that everyone else would probably say is just normal.\n\nLike those tickets — little marks, weird notes near the totals — and then the servers standing near the register, and that regular customer just sitting there watching. To me it feels like they’re leaving signs, like a trail, to show I rang something in wrong so they can get me fired.\n\nAnd I know my husband would say, “That’s not a real thing, nobody’s doing that, it’s just restaurant mess-ups,” and maybe most people would think that sounds strange. But when I’m there, in the middle of it, it feels real. Like I’m the only one noticing it.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was happening that you know most people would think is impossible or doesn’t happen in real life?\n\nPatient: I guess… not impossible like flying or something. But, yeah, I keep getting this feeling at work that a couple of the servers and this one regular customer are watching how I ring things in. Like they’re not just looking over by accident.\n\nAnd then if there are little marks or weird notes on the tickets, I start thinking they’re leaving signs, like proof that I did something wrong so they can get me fired. My husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, people mess up tickets all the time, and I know most people would probably say that doesn’t really happen that way.\n\nBut when I’m there, it feels like it’s happening. It feels obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that you yourself would describe as completely outside the realm of normal possibility — something that no one else could ever experience or that goes entirely against how the world works?\n\nPatient: Completely outside… no, I don’t think like, laws of the universe or whatever. I’m not saying something impossible like that. It’s more… at work, I keep feeling like a couple of the servers and that one regular customer are watching me when I ring things in, and then there’ll be these little marks on the tickets, like they’re leaving signs or keeping proof so I get in trouble.\n\nAnd I know my husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, like people scribble on tickets and mistakes happen, and he makes it sound like I’m turning it into something. But when I’m there, it feels connected. Like the ticket, the way someone stands near the register, the customer just sitting with his coffee forever… I mean, he always orders the same thing too, which shouldn’t matter, I know, but it starts to feel like it does.\n\nSo not impossible exactly. Just something most people probably wouldn’t see the way I do.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was happening that you yourself would describe as completely outside the realm of normal possibility — something that no one else could ever experience or that goes entirely against how the world works?\n\nPatient: I mean… completely outside normal possibility, no, not like something nobody could ever experience. It’s not like I think the world stopped working or anything.\n\nIt’s more that at work I get stuck on this thing with the tickets. Like I’ll ring something in, and then there’ll be a little mark near the total, or some note that doesn’t really need to be there, and one of the servers will be hanging around the register. And there’s this regular customer too, he’s there a lot, and I start feeling like they’re watching how I do it, like they’re leaving little signs to prove I messed up so they can get me fired.\n\nMy husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, you know, tickets get messy, people scribble things, mistakes happen. And I can hear that when he says it. But when I’m actually there, it doesn’t feel normal. It feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if any of these beliefs you’ve had—like people leaving signs on tickets or watching you at work—felt like something that could happen in real life, or did they feel more like something unusual or not based on how things normally work?\n\nPatient: It feels like it could happen, I guess. Like… people do watch each other at work, and people do write things on tickets, so it’s not like magic or anything. But then it gets bigger in my head.\n\nLike if one of the servers is near the register, and there’s some weird mark on a ticket, I start thinking, okay, they’re tracking what I’m doing, they’re leaving proof so later they can say I messed up and get me fired. And there’s that regular customer too, just sitting there, and I know customers sit around all the time, but with him it feels like he’s part of it somehow.\n\nMy husband keeps saying it’s just normal restaurant mess-ups, and maybe it is, but when I’m in the middle of it, it doesn’t feel normal. It feels really pointed at me.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been times when you were absolutely certain about something happening—like people trying to get you in trouble—even though there was no real evidence, and it turned out not to be true at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, there have been times I felt really sure. Like at work, with the servers and the tickets. I’ll see a mark near the total or something written in a way that seems unnecessary, and if one of them is nearby, it feels like they’re keeping track of me, like they’re making a little record so later they can say I did something wrong and get me fired.\n\nAnd there’s this regular customer who’s there a lot, and I know that sounds kind of dumb because people are allowed to sit and drink coffee, obviously, but he just… he watches, or it feels like he watches. It reminds me of when I was in school and people would pass notes, and you knew it was about you even if nobody said your name—sorry, that’s not really the same thing.\n\nMy husband says there’s no real evidence, that tickets get marked up all the time and restaurants are messy and fast. And later, sometimes, I can see that maybe nothing actually happened. But in the moment, I’m pretty convinced it’s all connected and meant to get me in trouble.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you think your concerns about people watching you or leaving signs on tickets are actually happening in real life, or if they’re more about how you feel in the moment, even if they might not make sense to someone else?\n\nPatient: I think… when I’m calm, like at home talking to my husband, I can say maybe it’s just how it feels in the moment. Like restaurants are chaotic, tickets get marked up, people stand around the register because that’s where things happen.\n\nBut when I’m actually there, it feels real. Like a couple of the servers are watching how I ring things in, and then there’ll be a little mark on the ticket or some note, and I’ll think, see, that’s not random. That’s them leaving signs, like proof, so later they can say I did something wrong and get me fired.\n\nAnd there’s this regular customer who’s always there, or it feels like he’s always there, and I know regulars exist—my mom used to go to the same diner every Thursday with her friend, so I know people have routines—but with him it feels like he’s part of it too. My husband says it’s just normal mess-ups and I’m making connections that aren’t there, and maybe someone else would see it that way. But in the moment, it really feels like it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that something very unusual was happening—something that you know most people would find hard to believe or that doesn't happen in real life, even if it felt completely true to you at the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I don’t know if it’s “very unusual” like, not real-life unusual, because it feels like it could be real. But I guess other people would probably think it sounds… hard to believe.\n\nAt work I keep thinking a couple of the servers are watching how I ring things in, and then there’ll be these little marks or signs on the tickets, like they’re leaving proof. Like they’re building a case or something so they can say I messed up and get me fired. And there’s this regular customer too, and he’ll be sitting there, and I start feeling like he’s part of it.\n\nMy husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, like tickets get messy and people stand by the register. And I know restaurants are like that—I mean, half the time the printer jams or somebody writes something weird because they’re in a rush—but when I’m there, it feels completely true. Like it’s aimed at me.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been times when you remained completely certain about something happening—like people trying to get you in trouble—even when there was clear evidence it wasn’t true at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess that’s the part my husband gets frustrated with, because he’ll show me that it’s just a regular ticket mark or that a server writes on everybody’s tickets, not just mine. And I can sort of hear that, but at work I still feel completely sure they’re watching how I ring things in.\n\nLike a couple of the servers and this one regular customer… it just feels connected. The customer sits there, and then the servers are near the register, and then there’s some little sign or mark on the ticket, and my brain goes, that’s it, they’re making proof so they can get me fired.\n\nEven if my husband says it’s normal restaurant mess-ups—like, he used to work in a pizza place for one summer and he always brings that up, which isn’t the same but okay—when I’m in it, I don’t really believe him. Later maybe I can calm down and think maybe I overread it, but in the moment, yes, I stay really certain.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was happening that you yourself recognized as not making sense or being something no one else would understand as realistic?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, sometimes I do know it sounds like it doesn’t make sense when I say it out loud. Like the thing with the tickets at work—me thinking a couple of the servers, and that one regular customer, are watching how I ring things in and leaving little signs on the tickets so they can prove something and get me fired.\n\nWhen I’m saying it here, I can hear how someone would be like, “Why would they do all that?” My husband says it’s just normal restaurant mess-ups, like people scribbling on tickets or standing near the register because it’s busy. And he’ll say I’m making it into something bigger.\n\nBut when I’m there, it doesn’t feel unrealistic. It feels like… obvious, almost. Like the marks mean something. Sorry, I know I’m kind of going in circles—restaurants are just so hectic, and people do weird little things all shift long—but yeah, there are times I recognize other people wouldn’t understand it as realistic, even if part of me still feels like it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was happening that you yourself would describe as completely illogical or impossible for anyone else to experience, even if it felt completely true to you at the time?\n\nPatient: Completely impossible… I don’t know if I’d call it impossible, because that makes it sound like I know it can’t happen. It’s more like, when I’m at work, it feels completely true, but then later if I explain it, I can hear how it sounds kind of… illogical.\n\nLike I keep thinking a couple of the servers and this one regular customer are watching how I ring things in, and then the little marks on the tickets are like signs, like they’re trying to prove I’m doing something wrong so they can get me fired. Like restaurants are chaotic, tickets get marked up, people stand around the register because that’s where things happen.\n\nBut when I’m actually there, it feels real. Like a couple of the servers are watching how I ring things in, and then there’ll be a little mark on the ticket or some note, and I’ll think, see, that’s not random. That’s them leaving signs, like proof, so later they can say I did something wrong and get me fired.\n\nAnd there’s this regular customer who’s always there, or it feels like he’s always there, and I know regulars exist—my mom used to go to the same diner every Thursday with her friend, so I know people have routines—but with him it feels like he’s part of it too. My husband says it’s just normal mess-ups and I’m making connections that aren’t there, and maybe someone else would see it that way. But in the moment, it really feels like it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that something very unusual was happening—something that you know most people would find hard to believe or that doesn't happen in real life, even if it felt completely true to you at the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I don’t know if it’s “very unusual” like, not real-life unusual, because it feels like it could be real. But I guess other people would probably think it sounds… hard to believe.\n\nAt work I keep thinking a couple of the servers are watching how I ring things in, and then there’ll be these little marks or signs on the tickets, like they’re leaving proof. Like they’re building a case or something so they can say I messed up and get me fired. And there’s this regular customer too, and he’ll be sitting there, and I start feeling like he’s part of it.\n\nMy husband says it’s just normal restaurant stuff, like tickets get messy and people stand by the register. And I know restaurants are like that—I mean, half the time the printer jams or somebody writes something weird because they’re in a rush—but when I’m there, it feels completely true. Like it’s aimed at me.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been times when you remained completely certain about something happening—like people trying to get you in trouble—even when there was clear evidence it wasn’t true at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess that’s the part my husband gets frustrated with, because he’ll show me that it’s just a regular ticket mark or that a server writes on everybody’s tickets, not just mine. And I can sort of hear that, but at work I still feel completely sure they’re watching how I ring things in.\n\nLike a couple of the servers and this one regular customer… it just feels connected. The customer sits there, and then the servers are near the register, and then there’s some little sign or mark on the ticket, and my brain goes, that’s it, they’re making proof so they can get me fired.\n\nEven if my husband says it’s normal restaurant mess-ups—like, he used to work in a pizza place for one summer and he always brings that up, which isn’t the same but okay—when I’m in it, I don’t really believe him. Later maybe I can calm down and think maybe I overread it, but in the moment, yes, I stay really certain.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was happening that you yourself recognized as not making sense or being something no one else would understand as realistic?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, sometimes I do know it sounds like it doesn’t make sense when I say it out loud. Like the thing with the tickets at work—me thinking a couple of the servers, and that one regular customer, are watching how I ring things in and leaving little signs on the tickets so they can prove something and get me fired.\n\nWhen I’m saying it here, I can hear how someone would be like, “Why would they do all that?” My husband says it’s just normal restaurant mess-ups, like people scribbling on tickets or standing near the register because it’s busy. And he’ll say I’m making it into something bigger.\n\nBut when I’m there, it doesn’t feel unrealistic. It feels like… obvious, almost. Like the marks mean something. Sorry, I know I’m kind of going in circles—restaurants are just so hectic, and people do weird little things all shift long—but yeah, there are times I recognize other people wouldn’t understand it as realistic, even if part of me still feels like it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something was happening that you yourself would describe as completely illogical or impossible for anyone else to experience, even if it felt completely true to you at the time?\n\nPatient: Completely impossible… I don’t know if I’d call it impossible, because that makes it sound like I know it can’t happen. It’s more like, when I’m at work, it feels completely true, but then later if I explain it, I can hear how it sounds kind of… illogical.\n\nLike I keep thinking a couple of the servers and this one regular customer are watching how I ring things in, and then the little marks on the tickets are like signs, like they’re trying to prove I’m doing something wrong so they can get me fired. And my husband will say, “That’s just restaurant stuff, tickets get messy,” and he’s probably right in a normal way, but—sorry, the ticket printer at our place is awful, it jams all the time and everybody blames everybody for it—but the marks don’t feel random to me when I’m there.\n\nSo yeah, I guess there are times where part of me knows other people would not experience it that way, or would say it doesn’t make sense. But in the moment it feels like it’s happening right in front of me."
